I have a thorough understanding of the codebase now. Let me design the implementation plan.

---

## Triage Disposition Map Refactor -- Implementation Plan

### Overview

The core change: introduce an `issue_dispositions` dict in `epic_triage_meta` that serves as the single shared per-issue state across all triage stages. Each stage reads accumulated state and writes its own fields. Non-genuine observe verdicts immediately create skip entries, removing them from the pipeline before reflect sees them.

### Answer to Design Questions

**1. Phasing**: Three phases is the right approach. The disposition map is a structural change to the data model that needs to land cleanly before building auto-skip logic on top of it (auto-skip changes control flow in observe, which touches prompts, skip operations, and downstream filtering). Legacy cleanup is always last.

**2. Migration**: The existing `triage_stages.observe.assessments` and `triage_stages.reflect.disposition_ledger` data can be migrated opportunistically. Since triage is episodic (you complete one round, then start a fresh one), mid-triage migration is the only concern. The pragmatic path: if an active triage has old-format data, upgrade it lazily when the next stage runs. No `upgrade_plan_to_v9` needed -- the disposition map is additive within `epic_triage_meta`, and `ensure_plan_defaults` already handles missing keys. PLAN_VERSION stays at 8.

**3. Stage reports**: Yes, keep them. Reports remain in `triage_stages[stage].report` as human-readable narrative. The disposition map is the authoritative machine-readable state. Reports become read-only context; validation reads the map, not the report text.

**4. Cascade clear**: When observe is re-run, it rewrites `issue_dispositions` verdicts for all issues. The cascade function should also clear `decision`, `placed`, `enriched`, `verified` fields from every disposition entry (since those were written by later stages). This is cleaner than the current approach of clearing confirmation timestamps on stage dicts.

**5. Prompt builders**: The `_format_assessments_table()` function in `stage_prompts.py` should be replaced by `_format_disposition_table()` which shows the full accumulated state per issue. For reflect prompts, it shows verdicts + recommendations. For organize prompts, it shows verdicts + decisions + targets. Each stage gets a view of all prior-stage data.

---

### Phase 1: Disposition Map Data Structure

**Goal**: Add `issue_dispositions` to `epic_triage_meta` and have observe/reflect write to it, in parallel with existing structures. No behavior changes yet. This is the foundation.

#### 1a. Schema additions

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/__init__.py`

Add a new TypedDict:

```python
class IssueDisposition(TypedDict, total=False):
    """Per-issue triage state accumulated across stages."""
    # Observe writes:
    verdict: str           # "genuine" | "false positive" | "exaggerated" | "over-engineering" | "not worth it"
    verdict_reasoning: str
    files_read: list[str]
    recommendation: str
    
    # Reflect writes (genuine issues only):
    decision: str          # "cluster" | "skip"
    target: str            # cluster name or skip reason
    
    # Organize confirms:
    placed: bool
    
    # Enrich confirms:
    enriched: bool
    
    # Sense-check confirms:
    verified: bool
```

Add to `EpicTriageMeta`:
```python
issue_dispositions: dict[str, IssueDisposition]  # keyed by issue_id
```

Add `"issue_dispositions"` key initialization in default empty triage meta (handled naturally by `meta.setdefault("issue_dispositions", {})`).

#### 1b. Observe writes to disposition map

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/observe.py`

After building the `assessments` list (line ~106-115), also populate the disposition map:

```python
dispositions = meta.setdefault("issue_dispositions", {})
for entry in evidence.entries:
    full_id = short_map.get(entry.issue_hash) or entry.issue_hash
    dispositions[full_id] = {
        "verdict": entry.verdict,
        "verdict_reasoning": entry.verdict_reasoning,
        "files_read": entry.files_read,
        "recommendation": entry.recommendation,
    }
```

This requires `_build_short_map` from `evidence_parsing.py` to resolve short hashes to full IDs. The `short_map` is already built inside `parse_observe_evidence` but not returned. Either: (a) refactor to return it, or (b) build it again in observe.py using the same `_build_short_map` function. Option (b) is simpler and the function is cheap.

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/records.py`

`record_observe_stage` does not need to change yet -- it still writes `assessments` to the stage payload for backward compat.

#### 1c. Reflect writes to disposition map

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/reflect.py`

In `_persist_reflect_stage`, after writing `disposition_ledger` to the stage payload, also update the disposition map:

```python
dispositions = meta.setdefault("issue_dispositions", {})
for d in disposition_ledger:
    entry = dispositions.setdefault(d.issue_id, {})
    entry["decision"] = "skip" if d.decision == "permanent_skip" else d.decision
    entry["target"] = d.target
```

Note the normalization: `permanent_skip` in the ledger becomes `skip` in the disposition map. This simplifies downstream checks.

#### 1d. Cascade clear updates disposition map

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stage_queue.py`

Add a new function `cascade_clear_dispositions` that clears downstream fields from the disposition map when a stage is re-run:

```python
_STAGE_DISPOSITION_FIELDS = {
    "observe": ["verdict", "verdict_reasoning", "files_read", "recommendation", 
                 "decision", "target", "placed", "enriched", "verified"],
    "reflect": ["decision", "target", "placed", "enriched", "verified"],
    "organize": ["placed", "enriched", "verified"],
    "enrich": ["enriched", "verified"],
    "sense-check": ["verified"],
}

def cascade_clear_dispositions(meta: dict, from_stage: str) -> None:
    """Clear disposition fields written by from_stage and all later stages."""
    dispositions = meta.get("issue_dispositions", {})
    if not dispositions:
        return
    fields_to_clear = _STAGE_DISPOSITION_FIELDS.get(from_stage, [])
    for entry in dispositions.values():
        for field in fields_to_clear:
            entry.pop(field, None)
```

Call this from `record_observe_stage` (and the other record functions) alongside the existing `cascade_clear_later_confirmations`.

For observe specifically: re-running observe clears the entire disposition map since all data is being regenerated. Simpler to just do `meta["issue_dispositions"] = {}`.

#### 1e. Tests

Add tests verifying:
- Observe populates `issue_dispositions` with correct verdicts for each issue
- Reflect populates `decision` and `target` for each dispositioned issue
- Re-running observe clears downstream disposition fields
- Re-running reflect clears organize/enrich/sense-check fields
- Existing `assessments` and `disposition_ledger` still populated (backward compat)

---

### Phase 2: Auto-skip for Non-genuine Verdicts

**Goal**: When observe records a non-genuine verdict (false-positive, exaggerated, over-engineering, not-worth-it), the system immediately creates skip entries and marks `decision: "skip"` + `placed: True` in the disposition map. Reflect only sees genuine issues.

#### 2a. Observe auto-skip logic

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/observe.py`

After populating the disposition map, partition issues:

```python
NON_GENUINE_VERDICTS = frozenset({"false positive", "exaggerated", "over-engineering", "not worth it"})

auto_skip_ids = []
for full_id, disp in dispositions.items():
    if disp.get("verdict") in NON_GENUINE_VERDICTS:
        disp["decision"] = "skip"
        disp["target"] = disp["verdict"]  # reason = the verdict itself
        disp["placed"] = True
        auto_skip_ids.append(full_id)
```

Then create actual skip entries:

```python
if auto_skip_ids:
    from desloppify.engine._plan.operations.skip import skip_items, SkipOptions
    skip_items(plan, auto_skip_ids, options=SkipOptions(
        kind="false_positive" if verdict == "false positive" else "permanent",
        reason=f"Triage observe: {verdict}",
        note=verdict_reasoning,
        scan_count=state.get("scan_count", 0),
    ))
```

Actually, the skip kind should be per-issue. Better to call `skip_items` per verdict group, or inline the skip entry creation. Since each issue may have a different verdict, iterate:

```python
for full_id in auto_skip_ids:
    disp = dispositions[full_id]
    kind = "false_positive" if disp["verdict"] == "false positive" else "permanent"
    skip_items(plan, [full_id], options=SkipOptions(
        kind=kind,
        reason=f"Triage observe: {disp['verdict']}",
        note=disp.get("verdict_reasoning"),
        scan_count=state.get("scan_count", 0),
    ))
```

Print feedback: `"  Auto-skipped {len(auto_skip_ids)} non-genuine issue(s)."`.

#### 2b. Reflect filters to genuine-only

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/reflect.py`

In `_validate_reflect_submission`, after collecting `valid_ids`, filter out issues that already have `placed: True` in the disposition map:

```python
dispositions = meta.get("issue_dispositions", {})
already_placed = {iid for iid, d in dispositions.items() if d.get("placed")}
reflect_ids = valid_ids - already_placed
```

Use `reflect_ids` instead of `valid_ids` for accounting validation. This means the reflect report only needs to account for genuine issues.

Update the reflect prompt builder to communicate this: "The following N issues have already been dispositioned by observe (non-genuine). You only need to address the remaining M genuine issues."

#### 2c. Coverage tracking simplification

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/review_coverage.py`

`undispositioned_triage_issue_ids` currently checks clusters + skipped + dismissed. With the disposition map, add a check for `placed: True`:

```python
dispositions = ensure_triage_meta(plan).get("issue_dispositions", {})
placed_ids = {iid for iid, d in dispositions.items() if d.get("placed")}
covered_ids.update(placed_ids)
```

This is additive -- it augments the existing checks, so it works whether the auto-skip already created a skip entry or not.

#### 2d. Organize validates disposition map

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/validation/organize_policy.py`

`validate_organize_against_reflect_ledger` currently reads `stages["reflect"]["disposition_ledger"]`. Add a secondary validation path that reads the disposition map:

```python
def validate_organize_against_dispositions(*, plan: dict, meta: dict) -> list[LedgerMismatch]:
    """Validate plan state against the disposition map."""
    dispositions = meta.get("issue_dispositions", {})
    actuals = _build_actual_disposition_index(plan)
    unplaced = ActualDisposition(kind="unplaced")
    mismatches = []
    for issue_id, disp in dispositions.items():
        decision = disp.get("decision")
        if not decision:
            continue  # observe-only, no decision yet
        if disp.get("placed"):
            continue  # already confirmed
        target = disp.get("target", "")
        intended = ReflectDisposition(issue_id=issue_id, decision=decision, target=target)
        actual = actuals.get(issue_id, unplaced)
        if not _disposition_matches(intended, actual):
            mismatches.append(LedgerMismatch(intended=intended, actual=actual))
    return mismatches
```

For now, keep both validation paths. The ledger-based validation runs if no disposition map exists (backward compat). Disposition-map validation runs when the map is present.

#### 2e. Organize marks `placed: True`

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/organize.py`

In `_persist_organize_stage`, after recording the stage, mark all issues with a `decision` as `placed`:

```python
dispositions = meta.get("issue_dispositions", {})
for issue_id, disp in dispositions.items():
    if disp.get("decision") and not disp.get("placed"):
        # Verify it's actually in plan state
        actual = actuals.get(issue_id)
        if actual and actual.kind != "unplaced":
            disp["placed"] = True
```

```python
for disp in dispositions.values():

```

Similarly for sense-check: mark `verified: True`.

#### 2g. Prompt builder updates

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/stage_prompts.py`

Replace `_format_assessments_table` with `_format_disposition_table` that shows the disposition map state appropriate for the target stage:

- For organize: show decision + target for each issue

Update `_relevant_prior_reports` to include disposition table instead of (or in addition to) the assessments table.

#### 2h. Tests

- Test that observe with non-genuine verdicts creates skip entries immediately
- Test that reflect's `valid_ids` excludes auto-skipped issues
- Test that `undispositioned_triage_issue_ids` counts disposition-map placed issues
- Test organize validation against disposition map
- Test that organize marks `placed: True`
- Test that re-running observe clears auto-skip entries (unskip) and regenerates

### Phase 3: Legacy Removal

**Goal**: Remove redundant data structures now that the disposition map is authoritative.

#### 3a. Remove `assessments` from `TriageStagePayload`

The `assessments` list on the observe stage payload is fully redundant with the disposition map. Remove:

- The `assessments` field from `TriageStagePayload` TypedDict
- The `assessments` parameter from `record_observe_stage`
- The assessments-building code in `observe.py` (lines 106-115)
- `_format_assessments_table` and `_format_assessment_file_evidence` from `stage_prompts.py`

Keep backward compat: if loading old plan data that has `assessments` but no `issue_dispositions`, lazily migrate:

```python
def _migrate_assessments_to_dispositions(meta: dict) -> None:
    """One-time migration from old assessments to disposition map."""
    stages = meta.get("triage_stages", {})
    assessments = stages.get("observe", {}).get("assessments", [])
    if not assessments or meta.get("issue_dispositions"):
        return
    dispositions = {}
    for a in assessments:
        # Can't fully resolve issue_id from short hash without valid_ids,
        # so store by hash as a best-effort fallback
        dispositions[a["hash"]] = {
            "verdict": a.get("verdict", ""),
            "verdict_reasoning": a.get("verdict_reasoning", ""),
            "files_read": a.get("files_read", []),
            "recommendation": a.get("recommendation", ""),
        }
    meta["issue_dispositions"] = dispositions
```

#### 3b. Remove `disposition_ledger` from `TriageStagePayload`

Similarly redundant. Remove:

- `disposition_ledger` and `cluster_blueprint` fields from `TriageStagePayload`
- `ReflectDisposition` and `ReflectClusterBlueprint` TypedDicts from schema (keep the dataclass in `reflect_accounting.py` for parsing)
- The ledger-writing code in `_persist_reflect_stage`

#### 3c. Simplify organize validation

Remove `validate_organize_against_reflect_ledger` (the old ledger-based path). Keep only `validate_organize_against_dispositions`. Update `_validate_organize_against_ledger_or_error` to call the disposition-based validation.

#### 3d. Simplify `ReflectDisposition` dataclass

The `ReflectDisposition` dataclass in `reflect_accounting.py` is still needed for parsing the Coverage Ledger section from report text. But `from_dict`/`to_dict` for persistence are no longer needed since we write to the disposition map directly.

---

### Implementation Sequencing

1. **Phase 1** (foundation, ~2 sessions): Schema + write paths + cascade clear. All existing tests should pass since this is additive. New tests validate the map is populated.

2. **Phase 2** (behavior change, ~2-3 sessions): Auto-skip + filtering + validation. This changes user-visible behavior. Existing tests that assert on `undispositioned_triage_issue_ids` or organize validation may need updates. Run the full test suite incrementally.

3. **Phase 3** (cleanup, ~1 session): Remove redundant structures. This is mostly deletion. Tests that reference `assessments` or `disposition_ledger` directly will need rewriting.

### Risk Factors

- **Test count**: ~34 triage-specific test files. Phase 2 will likely touch 10-15 of them since it changes validation behavior.
- **Mid-triage migration**: A user with an in-progress triage (observe done, reflect pending) will get a disposition map populated on next observe or reflect run. The old `assessments` data continues to work as prompt context. No data loss.
- **Skip entry idempotency**: Auto-skip in observe must handle re-runs gracefully. If observe is re-run, existing auto-skip entries should be removed first (unskip), then re-created based on new verdicts. This is the trickiest part.
- **Prompt quality**: The prompt builders currently format assessments as a table for downstream LLM stages. The disposition table needs to be at least as informative.
